### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0035:c001 · PDF 35**

3.3.1. Organization, Management, and Work Culture

**G242:p0035:c002 · PDF 35**

To develop a legitimate and long-lasting transformation, the climate crisis and resource availability risks must be mitigated at the highest level of the company and considered in every decision. The principles and orientations taken by the CSR must be reinforced and adapted accordingly. By committing to this transformation, management encourages their employees to actively contribute to a transformation meaningful enough to transcend the organization.

**G242:p0035:c003 · PDF 35**

This motivation toward change must be supported by the usual management tools such as the definition of objectives, profit-sharing, etc. Each actor must be aware of the strategic carbon reduction objective and include it in their daily activities. Operationally, objectives must be available at an individual level, but the commitment to the global climate goal must be, more than anything, collective.

**G242:p0035:c004 · PDF 35**

More informal methods can also be considered to create emulation. Good practices can be shared through guilds and ICT communities these are powerful recognition and motivation tools.

**G242:p0035:c005 · PDF 35**

The possibility for each business unit to measure the environmental footprint of its activity at any time is a key element of such a process. It enables each team, Agile project, co-worker, business activity, or ICT professional to act at their level by defining their own objectives and management indicators; see Section 3.6.

**G242:p0035:c006 · PDF 35**

The development of a work culture able to unify co-workers through strategic climate objectives is necessary. The more this culture is supported and carried by an exemplary management team, the more it will inspire all co-workers. It must especially lead to the development and adoption of principles, reflexes based on strong values such as frugality, leadership by example, the right to make mistakes (and learning by doing so), simplicity, the human factor, critical mindset, etc. ICT communities will have to be the driving force within organizations to lead and support the transition towards digital sustainability.

**G242:p0036:c000 · PDF 36**

Professionals working in the digital sector have a key role to play in the development of a sustainable digital culture. All the information system actors and especially those with the highest level of expertise must be involved. Thus, depending on their positions, they should:

**G242:p0036:c001 · PDF 36**

• Be included into education program plans and receive specific resources for the completion of their mission

**G242:p0036:c002 · PDF 36**

• Participate in the construction and follow-up of the various indicators for monitoring the impacts of the information system

**G242:p0036:c003 · PDF 36**

• Participate in the various information system steering and governance boards to ensure that projects and decisions are aligned with frugality objectives

**G242:p0036:c004 · PDF 36**

• Act as a referent regarding the new digital temperance practices at diverse levels (development, business activity, etc.) and along the whole information system lifecycle

**G242:p0036:c005 · PDF 36**

Additionally, because of their key role between ICT and business units, product owners will have to bridge a gap between two vastly different worlds to solve a common issue. Their daily activities will have to evolve to include sustainable digital objectives across their entire action range: it is up to them to question the environmental value and to establish a balance between business value versus environmental footprint.

**G242:p0036:c006 · PDF 36**

Finally, the processes enabling the transformation of an organization, such as the Enterprise Architecture (particularly suitable thanks to its systemic approach), and the tracking or governing bodies set up within the organization will have to be adapted to integrate digital sustainability an objective, as described in Section 3.4 and Section 3.9.

**G242:p0036:c007 · PDF 36**

Overall, strong sponsorship from director level, as part of the company’s strategy, is required beyond the ICT department.

</details>

<a id="3.3.2"></a>
## 技能与教育项目

原文：3.3.2. Skills and Education Programs。PDF 物理页：[36, 37]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

每个组织都必须实施宣导与教育流程，以支持所有同事理解战略性低碳目标与变革的必要性。能源—气候与资源稀缺问题，以及数字技术对这些扰动的“贡献”，都必须被理解。因此，诸如“气候拼图”（Climate Fresk）或“数字拼贴”（Digital Collages）² 之类的工作坊——由同事主持、指出日常行动等——是被推荐的做法，有益于生态系统的认知与质疑。尽管可以引入外部贡献者，但建议优先采用由同事主导的举措，以最大化采纳率与影响力。

这些教育项目不应只依赖 ICT 社群：每一位员工都必须受益于培训项目，以理解其使用方式、决策或需求表达所导致的 ICT 环境影响。为此，可以利用广泛传播的大规模开放在线课程（MOOC），如 AXA Climate School [AXA]，或在线沟通。一些组织在《通用数据保护条例》（GDPR）或网络犯罪相关风险方面开展的培训活动，就是很好的范例，可参照并改编用于可持续性。

让我们研究“Green Digital”的例子。在一家法国大型银行的子公司内，一名员工于 2019 年主动创建了一个宣讲与教育项目（关于正确使用方式的最佳实践工作坊），以提高员工与管理者对环境问题及反对数字浪费的认识并开展教育。他通过展示如何更好地使用数字工具来提出解决方案。该举措自 2019 年 11 月起被列为“企业承诺”计划的一部分，使 1,000 多名同事受到宣导。它现在是集团层面自 2020 年初建立的一系列举措的组成部分。

除宣导与教育项目之外，数字可持续性还要求发展和引入新技能，例如碳足迹核算、生态设计以及软件工艺（“少开发，但开发得更好”）。这些技能补充了 ICT 岗位已要求的技能，对架构师和高级开发人员尤其如此——他们的职责之一就是引导并实施 ICT 实践。这些技能必须被纳入人力资源流程，加入相关 ICT 社群岗位说明，并根据目标受众通过专门教育项目加以培养。

采购人员或采购专业人员，其活动覆盖组织碳足迹范围 3 的很大部分，必须在教育项目和技能方面得到特别关注，因为他们同时扮演着信息系统环境影响的“开方者”（“我们要求供应商做什么”）和“采集者”（收集分包过程影响的度量）两种角色。

在招聘层面，企业必须确保候选人的画像与技能符合其新需求——既招聘在这些议题上受过教育的新毕业生，也把与数字可持续性相关的问题纳入面向所有人的招聘流程，进而确保新同事能够适应新的工作文化以及正在进行的转型。

> **脚注**：气候拼图：climatefresk.org/；数字拼贴：digitalcollage.org/。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 教育项目应让每位员工受益，不应仅依靠ICT社群。
  - 适用边界：架构师专门技能培训不能替代全员意识培养。
  - PDF 36：These education programs should not rely on ICT communities: every single employee must benefit from a training program to understand the environmental impact of ICT resulting from their uses and their decisions or expressions of needs. To do so, it is possible to leverage widely broadcasted Massive Open Online Courses (MOOCs) like the AXA Climate School [AXA] or online communication. The training campaigns performed in some organizations regarding the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) or the cybercrime-related risks are good examples to follow and adapt for sustainability.
- 需发展碳核算、生态设计及软件工程等专门技能。
  - 适用边界：课程完成不等于胜任；应定义岗位任务、行为证据与能力差距。
  - PDF 37：Beyond raising awareness and education program initiatives, digital sustainability requires the development and involvement of new skills such as carbon footprint accounting, eco-design, and software craftsmanship (“develop less but develop better”). These skills complement those already required by ICT positions, especially for architects and senior developers, whose role is, among other things, to guide and implement ICT practices. They will have to be included in HR processes and added to job descriptions for the involved ICT communities and developed by specific education programs depending on the target audience.
- 采购专业人员需能处理覆盖大量Scope3的采购环境要求。
  - 适用边界：无供应商生命周期证据时不能仅以价格或品牌作绿色评定。
  - PDF 37：The buyers or procurement professionals, whose activity covers a large part of Scope 3 of the organization’s carbon footprint, must be subject to special attention in terms of education programs and skills because they play a role of both prescriber (“what we ask our suppliers for”) and collectors (gathered measurements of the subcontracting process impact) of the environmental impact of the information system.
- 招聘及HR需对齐新角色与技能要求。
  - 适用边界：参考岗位画像不是现有人员已合格或正式资格认证。
  - PDF 37：At the hiring level, it is essential that businesses ensure that candidates’ profiles and skills match their new needs, by hiring recent graduates that have been educated on these topics, but also by integrating into the recruitment process questions related to digital sustainability for everybody, then ensuring that new co-workers will be able to adapt to the new work culture and the ongoing transformation.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0036:c008 · PDF 36**

3.3.2. Skills and Education Programs

**G242:p0036:c009 · PDF 36**

Processes to raise awareness and educate must be implemented within each organization to support the understanding by all co-workers of the strategic low carbon objective and the need for change. The energy-climate and resource scarcity issues as well as the digital technology contribution to these disruptions must be understood. Therefore, workshops such as the “Climate Fresk” or the “Digital Collages”,[2] which are conferences led by co-workers, pointing out daily actions, etc. are recommended initiatives to benefit the ecosystem knowledge and challenge. Although it is possible to involve external contributors, it is recommended to prioritize initiatives led by co-workers to maximize adoption and impact.

**G242:p0036:c010 · PDF 36**

These education programs should not rely on ICT communities: every single employee must benefit from a training program to understand the environmental impact of ICT resulting from their uses and their decisions or expressions of needs. To do so, it is possible to leverage widely broadcasted Massive Open Online Courses (MOOCs) like the AXA Climate School [AXA] or online communication. The training campaigns performed in some organizations regarding the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR) or the cybercrime-related risks are good examples to follow and adapt for sustainability.

**G242:p0037:c000 · PDF 37**

Let us study the example of “Green Digital”. Within a subsidiary of a large French bank, an employee took the initiative in 2019 to create a presentation and education program (workshops on best practices about proper uses) to raise awareness and educate employees and managers about environmental issues and the fight against digital waste. He suggested solutions by showing how to better use digital tools. This initiative has been listed since November 2019 as part of the “company engagement” initiative, which made it possible to raise awareness across more than 1,000 co-workers. It is now a building block of a series of initiatives at the group level that has been set up since the beginning of 2020.

**G242:p0037:c001 · PDF 37**

Beyond raising awareness and education program initiatives, digital sustainability requires the development and involvement of new skills such as carbon footprint accounting, eco-design, and software craftsmanship (“develop less but develop better”). These skills complement those already required by ICT positions, especially for architects and senior developers, whose role is, among other things, to guide and implement ICT practices. They will have to be included in HR processes and added to job descriptions for the involved ICT communities and developed by specific education programs depending on the target audience.

**G242:p0037:c002 · PDF 37**

The buyers or procurement professionals, whose activity covers a large part of Scope 3 of the organization’s carbon footprint, must be subject to special attention in terms of education programs and skills because they play a role of both prescriber (“what we ask our suppliers for”) and collectors (gathered measurements of the subcontracting process impact) of the environmental impact of the information system.

**G242:p0037:c003 · PDF 37**

At the hiring level, it is essential that businesses ensure that candidates’ profiles and skills match their new needs, by hiring recent graduates that have been educated on these topics, but also by integrating into the recruitment process questions related to digital sustainability for everybody, then ensuring that new co-workers will be able to adapt to the new work culture and the ongoing transformation.

</details>

<a id="3.3.3"></a>
## 沟通计划

原文：3.3.3. Communication Plan。PDF 物理页：[37]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

尊重数字可持续性原则的内部沟通，必须反映各个层面的挑战与努力。它必须启动并维持走向数字可持续目标的运动与动力。它可以突出挑战（Lean-ICT 黑客松等）、赞扬值得注意的进展、活跃企业氛围，或在这一数字节制过程中支持社群。通过定期沟通并就组织的行动保持透明——例如碳足迹或用电量的演变，或对欧盟电子废弃物法规 [European Commission B] 的合规情况——可以强化“组织正在减少环境影响”的信念。

外部沟通将在企业之间形成良性竞争，并提升对具有环保意识的人才的吸引力。例如，它可以推广关于数字可持续举措的会议或聚会；可以由员工主导，以产生更多动力。基于开放知识原则建立公共社群，也是一个好选择。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 内部沟通本身需尊重数字可持续原则，并反映各层挑战和进展。
  - 适用边界：不以宣传材料替代测量结果。
  - PDF 37：Internal communication respecting digital sustainability principles must reflect the challenges and efforts made at every level. It must kick off and sustain the movement and motivation towards the goal of digital sustainability. It can highlight challenges (Lean-ICT hackathons, etc.), praise noteworthy progress, animate corporations, or support communities during this digital temperance process. Conviction that the organization is progressing at reducing environmental impact can be reinforced with regular communication and transparency about the actions of the organization, such as the evolution of the carbon footprint or electricity consumption or the compliance to the European Union E-waste rule [European Commission B].

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0037:c004 · PDF 37**

3.3.3. Communication Plan

**G242:p0037:c005 · PDF 37**

Internal communication respecting digital sustainability principles must reflect the challenges and efforts made at every level. It must kick off and sustain the movement and motivation towards the goal of digital sustainability. It can highlight challenges (Lean-ICT hackathons, etc.), praise noteworthy progress, animate corporations, or support communities during this digital temperance process. Conviction that the organization is progressing at reducing environmental impact can be reinforced with regular communication and transparency about the actions of the organization, such as the evolution of the carbon footprint or electricity consumption or the compliance to the European Union E-waste rule [European Commission B].

**G242:p0037:c006 · PDF 37**

External communication will lead to healthy emulation between companies and will lead to higher attractivity for environmentally conscious talents. For instance, it can promote conferences or meet- ups about digital sustainability initiatives. It can be led by employees to generate more motivation. The set-up of public communities based on the open-knowledge principle can also be a good option.

</details>

<a id="3.3.4"></a>
## 节俭与再生式创新

原文：3.3.4. Frugal and Regenerative Innovation。PDF 物理页：[38]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

挑战在于“如何用更少的能源和更少的资源做得同样好甚至更好，同时再生生物圈、从自然机制中获取灵感”。由于 ICT 处于数字转型的核心，这并不是要停止或放慢创新，而是改变其部分范式。

节俭式创新（Frugal Innovation）[Radjou & Prabhu, 2015] 是一种旨在开发高效解决方案的方法：去除复杂与冗余，尽可能少用资源，优先人际互动而非机器。低技术（low-tech）方法对于应对物理约束也有意义 [Bihouix, 2020]，可用于以低影响方式创新——在所提供的服务质量上不作任何妥协，并预先考虑产品及其使用的生命周期。它必须建立在价值观（简单、节制等）之上，并依靠每一种技能。ICT 专业人员有创新的意愿，并善于在一个非常动态的市场中应对反复出现的挑战。因此，组织的创新能力必须在每个层面展开：只要符合数字能源/资源节俭目标，每一条建议都必须被考虑。因此，必须实施相应的工具与方法，使创新得以涌现、部署并被接受——从一开始就纳入 lean-ICT 方法的方法与实践。此后，ICT 项目必须依据数字可持续性标准接受质疑与评估。

节俭式创新可以成为重大变化与颠覆的来源，尤其是在改变工作方式、简化流程或组织时：每一位同事都必须接受变革的必要性与变革本身。要发展数字可持续性方法，我们必须认识到：这不仅仅是优化当前的信息系统，而是改变方向、改变我们的思维方式。

再生式创新（Regenerative Innovation）是一个新兴概念，从自然界汲取灵感——自然本身是自我维持、协作且具有韧性的。其目的是创建能够再生已被人类发展破坏的生物圈的商业与经济，从而通过生物质碳捕集、恢复水循环和保护生物多样性来减缓气候变化 [Regenerative Innovation]。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 节俭与再生创新关注以更少能源和资源提供价值，并考虑生物圈再生。
  - 适用边界：不能只把低成本等同低环境影响。
  - PDF 38：The challenge is “how to do as well, or even better, with less energy and less resources, while regenerating the biosphere, getting inspiration from natural mechanisms”. Since ICT lies at the heart of Digital Transformation, it is not about stopping or slowing down innovation but changing part of its paradigm.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0038:c000 · PDF 38**

3.3.4. Frugal and Regenerative Innovation

**G242:p0038:c001 · PDF 38**

The challenge is “how to do as well, or even better, with less energy and less resources, while regenerating the biosphere, getting inspiration from natural mechanisms”. Since ICT lies at the heart of Digital Transformation, it is not about stopping or slowing down innovation but changing part of its paradigm.

**G242:p0038:c002 · PDF 38**

Frugal Innovation [Radjou & Prabhu, 2015] is an approach that aims to develop efficient solutions, devoid of sophistication and superfluousness, using as few resources as possible, favoring human interactions over machines. Low-tech approaches are also of interest to deal with physical constraints [Bihouix, 2020] and can be considered to innovate with low impact, without any compromises regarding the quality of the provided service and by anticipating the lifecycle of the offer and its use. It must be based on values (simplicity, temperance, etc.) and rely on every skill. The ICT professionals have an appetite to innovate and to cope with recurring challenges in a very dynamic market. The innovation capabilities of the organization must therefore be deployed at every level: every suggestion must be considered if it meets the digital energy/resource frugality objective. Therefore, it will be necessary to implement the tools and methods that will enable innovation to emerge, be deployed, and be accepted by integrating the methods and practices of the lean-ICT approach from the start. ICT projects will then have to be challenged and evaluated by considering digital sustainability criteria.

**G242:p0038:c003 · PDF 38**

Frugal Innovation can be a source of major changes and disruptions, especially when it is about changing the way of working and simplifying processes or organizations: every co-worker must accept the need for change and the change itself. To develop a digital sustainability approach, we must recognize that it is not just about optimizing the current information system, it is about changing direction and changing our way of thinking.

**G242:p0038:c004 · PDF 38**

Regenerative Innovation is an emerging concept that draws inspiration from nature, which is inherently self-sustaining, collaborative, and resilient. The purpose is to create businesses and economy that regenerate the biosphere that has been destroyed by human development, and thus mitigate climate change with biomass carbon capture, restored water cycle, and preserved biodiversity [Regenerative Innovation].

</details>

<a id="3.4.1"></a>
## IT 战略：在支持增长与管理相关风险之间

原文：3.4.1. IT Strategy: Between Supporting Growth and Managing Associated Risks。PDF 物理页：[38, 39]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

IT 战略是组织战略的数字体现：它由对增长和利润率改善的持续追求所驱动。视语境而定，它采取了多种形式：

- 通过流程数字化系统性地寻求效率；
- 通过大量计算与指标管理提供决策支持；
- 在销售与营销中以 Web 为主导；
- 发展全新的纯数字业务模式（数字转型、平台经济）。

据 Statista [ICT Statista]，这些战略得到了预算的支持，2005 年至 2022 年间预算增长约 **166%**。

由此产生的数字转型显著增加了企业面对数字相关风险的风险敞口，这些风险在 21 世纪初开始被处理。以成本控制、过时控制与技术债务、系统复杂性与可扩展性控制为驱动的合理化计划被启动。最后，网络安全的巨额投资在 2004 年至 2017 年间增长了 **35 倍** [Cybercrime]，这些风险如今直接在执行管理层受到监视。数字可持续性议题是走同一条路径的合适候选者。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 数字化支出与增长数字具有原文引用年代。
  - 适用边界：历史增长率不自动外推为本组织预算或未来容量。
  - PDF 39：These strategies have been supported by budgets that have increased around 166% between 2005 and 2022 according to Statista [ICT Statista].

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0038:c007 · PDF 38**

3.4.1. IT Strategy: Between Supporting Growth and Managing Associated Risks

**G242:p0038:c008 · PDF 38**

An IT strategy is the digital embodiment of an organization’s strategy: it is driven by a constant search for growth and improved margins. Depending on the context, it has taken various forms:

**G242:p0038:c009 · PDF 38**

• Systematic search for efficiency through the digitization of processes

**G242:p0038:c010 · PDF 38**

• Decision support through the multiplication of calculations and management of indicators

**G242:p0038:c011 · PDF 38**

• Predominance of the Web for sales and marketing

**G242:p0038:c012 · PDF 38**

• Development of new fully digital business models (Digital Transformations, platform economies)

**G242:p0039:c000 · PDF 39**

These strategies have been supported by budgets that have increased around 166% between 2005 and 2022 according to Statista [ICT Statista].

**G242:p0039:c001 · PDF 39**

The resulting Digital Transformation significantly increased companies’ exposure to digital-related risks that began to be addressed in the early 21st century. Rationalization programs driven by cost control, obsolescence control and technology debt, or system complexity and scalability control were initiated. Finally, massive investments in cybersecurity have increased 35-fold between 2004 and 2017 [Cybercrime], and these risks are now overseen directly at the executive management level. The digital sustainability topic is a suitable candidate to follow the same path.

</details>

<a id="3.4.2"></a>
## 数字技术相关的环境风险

原文：3.4.2. Environmental Risks Linked to Digital Technology。PDF 物理页：[39, 40, 41]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

“信息肥胖”（infobesity）一词用于表达企业因其不断增长的信息系统而无法快速适应新市场条件的风险。当面对环境约束时，它成为企业所面临气候风险的一个加重因素。

表 3-1 提出的分类法，是气候经济研究所（I4CE）为支持金融部门理解与气候变化相关的重大风险而提供的交付物 [Hubert et al., 2017]。在转型风险与物理气候风险之中，技术带来特定的转型风险，包括“数字技术的环境影响”。

**表 3-1：转型气候风险与物理气候风险的类型学（改编自 Hubert et al., 2017）**

**转型风险（Transition Risks）**

| 类别 | 内容 |
|---|---|
| 法规（Regulations） | 温室气体排放价格上涨（碳信用、税收等）；报告义务增加；对现有产品与服务的监管 |
| 市场（Markets） | 消费者行为变化；市场信号的不确定性；原材料成本上升 |
| 技术（Technologies） | 以排放更低的方案替代现有产品与服务；对新技术的投资失败；低碳新技术的初始开发成本；数字技术的环境影响 |
| 声誉（Reputation） | 消费者偏好变化；特定行业被污名化；利益相关者负面评论增加（如漂绿） |

**物理风险（Physical Hazards）**

| 类别 | 内容 |
|---|---|
| 急性（Acute） | 极端事件的强度与频率增加：气旋、飓风、洪水（造成设施损坏、产能下降） |
| 慢性（Chronic） | 天气模式变化与变异性增加；平均气温上升；海平面上升（造成设施损坏、运营成本上升、影响员工生产率） |

需要注意的是，该分类法仅限于能源—气候问题；而信息系统及其物理构建所引致的风险，还超出对有限矿产资源、水和土壤污染、社会与政治紧张的压力。

正如 TOGAF ADM 方法所建议的，该分类法可作为输入，用于识别业务转型风险与迁移活动，并评估初始与残余风险水平。

总之，企业高管对“信息肥胖”风险的理解，是可持续 IT 战略的第一步。

图3-3：风险、责任与CIO的角色；The Shift Project工作组产出。

相关结构化资料：[独立图示解析 fig3-3](../figures/G242/fig3-3.json)。图示模型、标签、关系与视觉状态单独维护；章节文本校订不升级图示审查状态。
相关结构化资料：[完整结构化表格](../tables/G242.json)。保留官方表空白、单位、示例状态及异常；实施契约在独立解析层补足。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 表3-1的风险分类限于能源与气候。
  - 适用边界：需按项目补充资源耗竭、污染、水与供应链风险，不能声称官方表已穷尽。
  - PDF 40：It should be noted that this taxonomy is limited to energy-climate issues, and that the risks induced by the Information System and its physical construction go beyond pressure on finite mineral resources, water and soil pollution, social and political tensions.
- 风险分类可作为业务转型风险识别及缓解输入。
  - 适用边界：风险类型存在不代表当前企业已受影响；需概率、影响和证据。
  - PDF 40：As proposed in the TOGAF ADM approach, this taxonomy can be an input to identify the Business Transformation Risks and Migration Activities assessing initial and residual level of risk.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0039:c002 · PDF 39**

3.4.2. Environmental Risks Linked to Digital Technology

**G242:p0039:c003 · PDF 39**

The term “infobesity” is used to express the risk for a company of not being able to adapt quickly to new market conditions because of its ever-growing Information System. When facing environmental constraints, it becomes an aggravating factor regarding the climatic risks that the company is facing.

**G242:p0039:c004 · PDF 39**

The taxonomy proposed in Table 3-1 is a deliverable of the Institute for Climate Economics to support the financial sector in the understanding of major risks associated with climate change [Hubert et al., 2017]. Among transitional and physical climate risks, technologies come with specific transition risk including “Environmental impact of digital technologies”.

**G242:p0040:c000 · PDF 40**

Table 3-1: Typology of Transitional and Physical Climate Risks. Adapted from Hubert et al., 2017

**G242:p0040:c001 · PDF 40**

| Transition Risks |  | Physical Hazards |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Regulations<br>• Increase in the price of GHG<br>emissions (carbon credits,<br>taxes…)<br>• Increase of reporting<br>obligations<br>• Regulations on existing<br>products and services | Markets<br>• Changes in consumer behavior<br>• Uncertainty of market signals<br>• Increase in raw materials costs | Acute<br>Increased intensity and frequency<br>of extreme events:<br>• Cyclones<br>• Hurricanes<br>• Floods<br>(Causes of damage on facilities,<br>reduction in production capacity.) |
| Technologies<br>• Substitution of existing<br>products and services with less<br>emissive options<br>• Unsuccessful investments in<br>new technologies<br>• Initial development costs of<br>new low-carbon technologies<br>• Environmental impact of<br>digital technology | Reputation<br>• Changes in consumer preferences<br>• Stigmatization of a particular sector<br>• Increase in negative comments from<br>stakeholders (e.g., greenwashing) | Chronic<br>• Changes in weather patterns<br>and increased variability<br>• Increase in average<br>temperatures<br>• Rising sea levels<br>(Causes of damage on facilities,<br>increased operating costs, impact<br>on employee productivity.) |

**G242:p0040:c002 · PDF 40**

It should be noted that this taxonomy is limited to energy-climate issues, and that the risks induced by the Information System and its physical construction go beyond pressure on finite mineral resources, water and soil pollution, social and political tensions.

**G242:p0040:c003 · PDF 40**

As proposed in the TOGAF ADM approach, this taxonomy can be an input to identify the Business Transformation Risks and Migration Activities assessing initial and residual level of risk.

**G242:p0040:c004 · PDF 40**

As a conclusion, the understanding of the “infobesity” risk by executives of the enterprise is the first step of a sustainable IT strategy.

**G242:p0041:c000 · PDF 41**

<a id="3.4.3"></a>
## 可持续 IT 战略：需要 CEO 赞助的负责任企业战略的表达

原文：3.4.3. Sustainable IT Strategy: Expression of a Responsible Corporate Strategy that Requires CEO Sponsorship。PDF 物理页：[41, 42]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

所有这些风险都有一个特点：远远超出企业的简单边界；而组织对其分担责任的认识不断提升，本身就是一个社会议题，并体现在公司的“使命声明”（mission statement）中。这一概念近来被法国 PACTE 法 [PACTE] 制度化，允许企业在其章程中“明确一项使命声明，由公司自设原则构成，并表明公司打算为实现其活动而投入手段予以遵守”。正如 BCG 2019 年可持续性报告 [BCG] 所述，这一使命声明对战略日益增长的影响正被越来越多地主张。

可持续 IT 战略是这一使命声明的数字面向。它要求对数字技术问题及其外部性给公司带来的成本采取整体方法。这必须作为一个整体来考虑：其外部约束、业务模式、最广义的组织（地点、流程、治理、职能）、其伙伴与供应商、其文化，当然还有其信息系统。在此意义上，转型的范围可与所谓的“数字”转型相比——后者同样需要强有力的赞助，并在执行委员会中有正式代表，例如以“首席数字可持续官”（Chief Digital Sustainability Officer）的形式。

这一可持续 IT 战略的愿景必须与 CSR 部门、风险管理以及业务单元共同构建：必须建立新的协同关系来定义它，并确保与其他环境方面（差旅、建筑等）、公司业务发展以及适用法规的整体一致性。

在法规这一最后议题上，与法务、可持续发展、审计以及负责为适用法规与标准整合碳报告的团队保持定期接触点，是一种良好实践。

在公司年报非财务章节中发布可持续性报告，将反映这一战略，并向生态体系传达企业战略。它应通过一个包含所有相关利益相关者的共识过程来交付。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 可持续IT战略应连接企业宗旨及高层责任。
  - 适用边界：法国PACTE法例子需保留辖区和历史语境，不能泛化为所有组织义务。
  - PDF 41：All these risks have the particularity of going far beyond the simple perimeter of the company, and the developing awareness of their share of responsibility as an organization is a societal issue, which is displayed in the company’s “mission statement”. This notion was recently institutionalized by the French PACTE law [PACTE], allowing companies to “specify a mission statement, consisting of the principles with which the company equips itself and for the respect of which it intends to allocate means in the realization of its activity” in their statutes. The growing influence of this mission statement on strategy is increasingly claimed, as explained in BCG’s 2019 sustainability report [BCG].
- 法务、可持续性、审计和风险团队应定期协调法规议题。
  - 适用边界：AI输出可提供检查清单，不可自授法律合规结论。
  - PDF 42：On this last topic of regulation, it is a good practice to have regular touchpoints with Legal, Sustainability, Audit, and teams in charge of carbon reporting consolidation for regulations and standards that apply.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0041:c001 · PDF 41**

3.4.3. Sustainable IT Strategy: Expression of a Responsible Corporate Strategy that Requires CEO Sponsorship

**G242:p0041:c002 · PDF 41**

All these risks have the particularity of going far beyond the simple perimeter of the company, and the developing awareness of their share of responsibility as an organization is a societal issue, which is displayed in the company’s “mission statement”. This notion was recently institutionalized by the French PACTE law [PACTE], allowing companies to “specify a mission statement, consisting of the principles with which the company equips itself and for the respect of which it intends to allocate means in the realization of its activity” in their statutes. The growing influence of this mission statement on strategy is increasingly claimed, as explained in BCG’s 2019 sustainability report [BCG].

**G242:p0041:c003 · PDF 41**

The strategy for sustainable IT is the digital side of this mission statement. It requires a holistic approach to the problem of digital technology and the cost of its externalities for the company. This must be considered as a whole: its external constraints, its business model, its organization in the broadest meaning – locations, processes, governance, functions – its partners and suppliers, its culture and, of course, its Information System. In this sense, the scope of the transformation is comparable to the so-called “Digital” Transformation one, which needed strong sponsorship as well and was officially represented at Executive Committees, in the form of a “Chief Digital Sustainability Officer” for example.

**G242:p0041:c004 · PDF 41**

The vision of this sustainable IT strategy must be built with the CSR department, Risk Management, and the Business Units: a new articulation must be set up to define it, and to ensure the overall cohesion with the other environmental aspects (travel, buildings, etc.), the development of the company’s activity and the applicable regulations.

**G242:p0042:c000 · PDF 42**

On this last topic of regulation, it is a good practice to have regular touchpoints with Legal, Sustainability, Audit, and teams in charge of carbon reporting consolidation for regulations and standards that apply.

**G242:p0042:c001 · PDF 42**

The publication of sustainability reporting in the non-financial chapter of a company’s annual report will reflect this strategy and inform the eco system of the enterprise strategy. It should be delivered through a consensual process involving all concerned stakeholders.

</details>

<a id="3.4.4"></a>
## 可持续数字战略的雄心

原文：3.4.4. Ambition of a Sustainable Digital Strategy。PDF 物理页：[42]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

确定这一战略的雄心，需要首先进行一次相当复杂的度量工作，旨在识别并量化公司当前数字环境足迹的整体。数字环境影响来自其直接用电（称为“范围 2”），但更主要地来自上下游的间接影响（称为“范围 3”）——从硬件制造与交付，到例如在外部供应商数据中心的运行——在全球层面，这占 ICT 温室气体排放的 **45%** [Shift Project, 2019]。

对这一全球足迹建模，并在公司内按价值链重新分配，将有助于第二项工作：定义减排目标、将其量化，并选择实现手段。任何转型项目群都必须就经济收益与环境影响之间的比率接受质疑。全球目标可与《巴黎协定》（COP21）相比较——该协定承诺全球排放减少 5% 至 7% [Shift Project, 2019]。在法国，国家低碳战略的雄心是到 2050 年将排放除以六 [SNBC, 2022]。

与任何战略的实施一样，需要由高层管理者确保的投资。各项举措可能产生正面或负面经济影响；尽管难以评估诸如精简信息系统、将供应商更换为更负责任者、把特定业务迁至能源结构更绿色的国家等如此多样行动的初始影响，但仍必须考虑其对公司盈利能力的潜在影响。在短期经济目标与环境目标之间寻求平衡，必须导向对运营预算可能增幅制定可接受的界限——这是必须明确表述的雄心限制因素。需要注意的是，确保此类战略融资的举措（资本支出（CapEx）与运营支出（OpEx）影响）正开始出现，例如内部碳税机制，按业务线的碳足迹向其收费。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 目标应基于含Scope2和Scope3的整体基线。
  - 适用边界：文中45%等份额使用不同范围/来源，不作为统一排放系数。
  - PDF 42：Determining the ambition of this strategy requires a first and rather complex measurement exercise that aims to identify and quantify the company’s current digital environmental footprint in its entirety. The digital environmental impact is due to its direct electricity consumption (known as “Scope 2”) but also and above all to the upstream and downstream indirect impacts (known as “Scope 3”) – from hardware manufacture and delivery to its use in an external supplier’s data center, for example – which at a worldwide level represents 45% of ICT GHG emissions [Shift Project, 2019].
- 按价值链分配足迹可支持制定适合组织的减排雄心。
  - 适用边界：源文5–7%未明确周期，不能自行变为每年5–7%的强制目标。
  - PDF 42：Modeling and redistributing this global footprint by value chain within the company will help in the second exercise of defining reduction objectives, quantifying them, and choosing the means to achieve them. Any transformation plan must be questioned regarding the ratio between economic gain and environmental impact. The global objectives can be compared with the Paris Agreement (COP21), which commits to a reduction in global emissions of 5% to 7% [Shift Project, 2019]. In France, the ambition of the National Low Carbon Strategy is to divide emissions by six by 2050 [SNBC, 2022].
- 长期投资需高层保障，即使短期可能有节省。
  - 适用边界：不得仅凭案例假定全部措施有正净现值。
  - PDF 42：As with the implementation of any strategy, investments secured by senior management are required. Initiatives may have positive or negative economic impacts, and while it is difficult to assess the initial impact of actions as diverse as streamlining the Information System, switching suppliers to more responsible ones, and relocating specific operations to countries with a greener energy mix, the potential impact on the company’s profitability must be considered. The search for a balance between short-term economic objectives and environmental targets must lead to the formulation of acceptable limits for the possible increase in the operating budget, a limiting factor of the ambition to be clearly formulated. It should be noted that initiatives to ensure the financing of such a strategy (Capital Expenditure (CapEx) and Operational Expenditure (OpEx) impact) start to emerge with internal carbon tax mechanisms, charging the business lines according to their carbon footprint.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0042:c002 · PDF 42**

3.4.4. Ambition of a Sustainable Digital Strategy

**G242:p0042:c003 · PDF 42**

Determining the ambition of this strategy requires a first and rather complex measurement exercise that aims to identify and quantify the company’s current digital environmental footprint in its entirety. The digital environmental impact is due to its direct electricity consumption (known as “Scope 2”) but also and above all to the upstream and downstream indirect impacts (known as “Scope 3”) – from hardware manufacture and delivery to its use in an external supplier’s data center, for example – which at a worldwide level represents 45% of ICT GHG emissions [Shift Project, 2019].

**G242:p0042:c004 · PDF 42**

Modeling and redistributing this global footprint by value chain within the company will help in the second exercise of defining reduction objectives, quantifying them, and choosing the means to achieve them. Any transformation plan must be questioned regarding the ratio between economic gain and environmental impact. The global objectives can be compared with the Paris Agreement (COP21), which commits to a reduction in global emissions of 5% to 7% [Shift Project, 2019]. In France, the ambition of the National Low Carbon Strategy is to divide emissions by six by 2050 [SNBC, 2022].

**G242:p0042:c005 · PDF 42**

As with the implementation of any strategy, investments secured by senior management are required. Initiatives may have positive or negative economic impacts, and while it is difficult to assess the initial impact of actions as diverse as streamlining the Information System, switching suppliers to more responsible ones, and relocating specific operations to countries with a greener energy mix, the potential impact on the company’s profitability must be considered. The search for a balance between short-term economic objectives and environmental targets must lead to the formulation of acceptable limits for the possible increase in the operating budget, a limiting factor of the ambition to be clearly formulated. It should be noted that initiatives to ensure the financing of such a strategy (Capital Expenditure (CapEx) and Operational Expenditure (OpEx) impact) start to emerge with internal carbon tax mechanisms, charging the business lines according to their carbon footprint.

</details>

<a id="3.4.5"></a>
## 战略的实施

原文：3.4.5. Implementation of the Strategy。PDF 物理页：[42, 43]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本文档提供一个框架，帮助定义将在公司所有职能中推展该战略的转型议程。该计划的高层“RACI”可简化如表 3-2 所示。

**表 3-2：RACI 的简化**

| R 负责（Responsible） | A 问责（Accountable） | C 咨询（Consulted） | I 知会（Informed） |
|---|---|---|---|
| 首席信息官（CIO） | 执行委员会中对以下主体拥有管辖权的成员：ICT 部门；首席执行官（CEO）；首席数字官（CDO）；首席运营官（COO） | CSR；业务专业人员；支持职能；合作伙伴；供应商 | 客户 |

请注意采购部门在该计划中日益重要的地位，这源于 IT 外包的规模以及“一切皆服务”（XaaS）市场的强劲趋势。与任何其他转型一样，该计划的成功取决于所有员工与伙伴的支持，以及其日常实践的转变，从而实现使这一范式转变长期持续的文化的改变。

相关结构化资料：[完整结构化表格](../tables/G242.json)。保留官方表空白、单位、示例状态及异常；实施契约在独立解析层补足。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 简化RACI中C为CSR、业务专业人员、支持职能、合作伙伴及供应商；I仅为客户。
  - 适用边界：R/A/C/I是不同责任类型；原文A栏层级措辞含糊，不据其虚构企业汇报关系。
  - PDF 43：| R | A | C | I |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Responsible | Accountable | Consulted | Informed |
| Chief Information Officer<br>(CIO) | Member of the Executive<br>Committee having authority<br>over:<br>The ICT Department<br>Chief Executive Officer (CEO)<br>Chief Digital Officer (CDO)<br>Chief Operations Officer (COO) | CSR<br>Business Professionals<br>Support Functions<br>Partners<br>Suppliers | Clients |
- 转型需要采购职能、员工与伙伴的持续支持和日常实践变化。
  - 适用边界：一个CIO角色或架构输出不足以实现跨企业能力。
  - PDF 43：Note the growing importance of the purchasing department within this program, due to the amount of IT outsourcing and the strong trend in the Everything as a Service (XaaS) market. Like any other, the success of this transformation program depends on the support of all employees and partners, and on the change of their daily practices to achieve the cultural change that will make this paradigm shift long-lasting and sustainable.

<a id="3.5.1"></a>
## 陈述可持续信息系统的愿景

原文：3.5.1. State the Vision of a Sustainable Information System。PDF 物理页：[43, 44, 45, 46]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

此处的目的是形成关于可持续信息系统应当是什么的高层、令人向往的愿景，并把力量汇聚到这一目标上。这一愿景是架构师的“电梯演讲”，将在赢得关键利益相关者支持方面发挥关键作用。

让我们给出一个可持续信息系统愿景的示例。由于目标信息系统可能与今天的样子截然不同，说明其未来形态及背后的理由十分重要。目的是提出一个令人向往、能够凝聚力量的可持续信息系统愿景。每个企业都应设计自己的愿景，或按下文所述的 2030 愿景加以调整以适配其业务语境。

2030 愿景要求公共政策、业务模式与 IT 部门组织发生重大变化，但在相当大的范围内技术上是可行的，并将长期使高质量的可持续数字服务成为可能。

**关于气候、能源、生物多样性、ICT 环境影响的教育**

全球变暖、生物多样性、水循环与碳循环、废弃物管理、能源供应等主题，连同最新 IPCC 报告的结论，被纳入每个学生的课程，从小学到大学。ICT 的环境影响，以及把个人使用保持在可控范围内的重要性（既为减少环境影响，也为心理健康与效率），也被广泛教授。企业还建立了学习旅程来培训员工，并使其了解最新发现——这得益于社会对这些议题关注度的提高。

**工作场所**

大多数员工配备单一设备，兼作个人与职业使用，并获得远程办公、互联网接入以及支撑日常活动的全套数字服务。

由于服务经过生态设计，它们现在所需的计算与连接资源非常有限，因此该设备只有智能手机大小，可插入提供舒适显示器、键盘与鼠标的工作站。标准现已使所有工作站、智能手机、充电器与耳机之间的兼容成为现实，并可获得用于移动设备电池充电的小型太阳能板。

这为移动办公以及在家或在办公室工作提供了完全的灵活性。大多数公共场所、企业和家庭也配备了工作站。

所有这些设备都经过用电优化，并且可以用一把标准螺丝刀轻松拆解和维修。只有在没有其他方案时才使用焊接或胶粘。孩子在 10 岁时就被教导如何更换电池；在大学阶段，学生被教导如何利用部分损坏设备的组件从零组装新设备。组件之间的高度标准化使复用变得容易，并最大限度减少回收需求。因此，全球数字垃圾大幅减少，采矿活动的强度显著下降，长期保障了资源存量，并最大限度减少了污染。

设备在设计上也减少了所需金属种类并便于回收，其中50%的金属现在得到回收。平均保修期为 15 年，如今的时尚是尽可能长时间地保留设备，最近的使用纪录是 25 年。已有计划将保修期延长至 20 年。

**数据中心/服务器**

由于数据中心的集中化，标准化发生得比工作场所更快。设备的平均保修期为 20 年，用电得到优化，数据中心的数量已稳定五年——这对保障电力供应起到了关键作用。事实上，电力生产中化石燃料的迅速退出需要对电厂和可再生能源进行大规模投资，这导致电力供应的稳定，而这一稳定与 2023 年仍能观察到的数据中心需求增长并不兼容。

**网络**

当电力危机发生时，社会不得不选择低能耗方案。光纤是首选的连接类型。移动连接只有一种统一方案，且不再需要任何升级即可满足不断增长的带宽需求，因为光纤集中承载了大部分家庭使用。电信运营商现在可以将精力投入在未覆盖区域部署网络，并在能源供应约束下维持高标准的质量服务。

所有用途也都实行了阶梯定价。用户起初难以把用量控制在预算之内，但如今已有许多习惯和方法来控制用量，几乎不需要为额外成本做规划，基本需求以极低成本即可满足。

**电力供应**

所有企业都对其数字用途进行了分类。当电网出现供应紧张时，应运营方的要求，组织可以在维持关键活动的同时减少用电需求。这对于缓解电价上涨也有帮助。

**操作系统管理**

操作系统现在是开源的、非常稳定，并由庞大且积极的开发者社区至少维护 10 年。这种稳定性对所有企业都是巨大收益——它们现在可以把 IT 资源投入改进自身业务特有的开发和安全工作。它还显著降低了对网络安全的需求，因为系统现在以成熟技术保障安全，不再有来自未更新操作系统或新代码的安全漏洞。

**软件**

软件厂商（editors）的强制维护期随时间延长，现已达到 20 年，与服务器的保修期对齐。为达到这一状态，与行业软件厂商进行了大量艰难的讨论，但这是延长设备使用时间的关键一点。此外，软件发布时必须在开源平台交付，以便在保修期结束时或软件公司停止运营时将其公开。

**数据管理**

任何新的结构化或非结构化数据都标注所有者、业务术语表索引与到期日，从而实现自动删除与存储量稳定。部分数据被标记为最终存储，并以开放标准格式归档在低能耗的备份方案上，从而保障未来的可访问性。

**数字服务**

数字服务的设计现在优化数据存储、用电、网络通信与处理，从而实现非常高的可访问性。一半的数字服务是开源的，另一半由运营方提供——其业务模式依托服务质量而非数据量。它们都内置了一些鼓励最少使用、并在数据量配额与使用时长超限时发出提醒的功能。它们被设计为有助于终结数字成瘾问题。这一变化在开始时由营销活动支撑，但如今习惯已经形成，不再需要这些活动。

**开放数据**

免费开放数据的数量已增长 1,000 倍，并以每年 5% 的速度增长。其管理由国际与国家级政府资助机构执行。这实现了高水平的互操作，同时减少了各组织自身的存储需求。

科学碳目标（SBT）倡议 [Science-Based Targets] 已扩大规模，基于科学的环境足迹与目标现在由联合国支持的独立国际组织为大多数组织认证，并维护这些目标的资料库。

**ICT 环境影响的度量**

环境足迹数据（温室气体排放、用电、水耗、对生物质与生物多样性的影响）现在与财务受到同等程度的重视并被跟踪，且可用于任何产品、服务乃至企业。标准与免费的参考共享数据在全球使用，大多数国家也设立了控制数据质量的机构。

得益于这一转变，ICT 的环境影响在大多数国家现已与碳中和目标兼容，同时提供高质量的数字服务，并支撑全球生物圈的密集再生。

**创新**

人工智能（AI）与区块链的炒作已被历史性的电力危机与自愿的使用限制所终止。创新现在专注于环境影响与能耗尽可能最低的生物数字（bio-digital）解决方案。DNA 数据存储能力、可回收并作为生物质燃料燃烧的生物聚合物电池，是年轻一代对完全可持续 IT 的希望。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 2030叙述是示范性愿景，应按企业业务语境自行设计或调整。
  - 适用边界：15/20/25年寿命、50%开源、1000倍开放数据和每年5%等全部保持示例标识。
  - PDF 43：Let us give an example of what a sustainable information system vision should be. As the target information system could be dramatically different to what it is today, it is important to explain its future shape and the rationale behind it. The purpose is to propose a desirable vision of a sustainable information system that can catalyze energy. Each enterprise should design its own vision or adapt the 2030 vision, described below, to its business context.
- 愿景中50%的金属得到回收。
  - 适用边界：不能译成50%的新产品金属来自回收；回收率与再生料含量不同。
  - PDF 44：Equipment have also been designed to reduce the number of metals required and to ease recycling, 50% of their metals are now recycled. The average guarantee is 15 years, and the fashion is now to keep devices as long as possible, the last record was a 25-year usage. There are plans to extend the guarantee to 20 years.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G242:p0043:c005 · PDF 43**

3.5.1. State the Vision of a Sustainable Information System

**G242:p0043:c006 · PDF 43**

The purpose here is to develop a high-level aspirational vision of what a sustainable information system should be, and to catalyze energy towards this goal. This vision is the architect’s elevator speech and will play a key role in gaining the buy-in of key stakeholders.

**G242:p0043:c007 · PDF 43**

Let us give an example of what a sustainable information system vision should be. As the target information system could be dramatically different to what it is today, it is important to explain its future shape and the rationale behind it. The purpose is to propose a desirable vision of a sustainable information system that can catalyze energy. Each enterprise should design its own vision or adapt the 2030 vision, described below, to its business context.

**G242:p0043:c008 · PDF 43**

The 2030 vision requires significant changes in public policies, business models and IT departments organizations but is technically feasible in a significant range and would enable sustainable quality digital services in the long term.

**G242:p0043:c009 · PDF 43**

Training on Climate, Energy, Biodiversity, ICT Environmental Impact

**G242:p0043:c010 · PDF 43**

Topics such as global warming, biodiversity, water and carbon cycles, waste management, energy supply, along the conclusions of the latest IPCC reports, are included in every student’s curriculum, from elementary school to university. ICT’s environmental impact and the importance of keeping personal usage under control, for environmental impact reduction as well as for mental health and

**G242:p0044:c000 · PDF 44**

efficiency is also taught widely. Enterprises have also set up learning journeys to train their employees and keep them updated on the latest findings, made possible by society’s increased focus on these topics.

**G242:p0044:c001 · PDF 44**

Workplace

**G242:p0044:c002 · PDF 44**

Most employees are equipped with a single device, both for personal and professional usage, offered remote working, access to the Internet and a full range of digital services to support their daily activities.

**G242:p0044:c003 · PDF 44**

As the services have been eco-designed, they require now very limited computing and connectivity resources, so this device is the size of a smartphone and can be plugged into a workstation providing a comfortable monitor, a keyboard, and a mouse. Standards now make compatibility between all workstations, smartphones, chargers, and headsets a reality, and small solar panels allowing mobile battery loading are available.

**G242:p0044:c004 · PDF 44**

This allows total flexibility for both mobility and work from home or at the office. Most public places, enterprise’s and homes are also equipped with workstations.

**G242:p0044:c005 · PDF 44**

All these equipment have an optimized electricity consumption and can be easily disassembled and repaired with a standard screwdriver. Welding or glue are used only if no other solution is possible. At 10 years old, children are taught how to change a battery. At university level, students are taught how to assemble a new device from scratch reusing components of partially broken devices. The high level of standardization between components allows easy reuse and minimizes the need for recycling. Consequently, digital dump has drastically reduced in the world and the intensity of mining activities has decreased significantly, securing stocks for the long term, and minimizing pollutions.

**G242:p0044:c006 · PDF 44**

Equipment have also been designed to reduce the number of metals required and to ease recycling, 50% of their metals are now recycled. The average guarantee is 15 years, and the fashion is now to keep devices as long as possible, the last record was a 25-year usage. There are plans to extend the guarantee to 20 years.

**G242:p0044:c007 · PDF 44**

Data Center / Servers

**G242:p0044:c008 · PDF 44**

Standardization has happened even faster than in the workspace thanks to the concentration of data centers. The average guarantee period of equipment is 20 years, electricity consumption is optimized, and the number of data centers has now stabilized for five years, which has been instrumental to secure electricity supply. Indeed, the swift exit of fossil fuels for electricity generation has required massive investment in power plants and renewables which has led to a stabilization of the electricity offer that was not compatible with the increase of data center needs still observed in 2023.

**G242:p0045:c000 · PDF 45**

Network

**G242:p0045:c001 · PDF 45**

When the electricity crisis happened, society had to choose low-energy solutions. Optic fiber is the preferred connection type. There is a unique solution for mobile connection that no longer needs any upgrade to meet increasing bandwidth demand since optic fiber is concentrating most of domestic uses. Telecom operators can now dedicate their effort to deploy network in non-covered area and maintain a high standard quality service under energy supply constraints.

**G242:p0045:c002 · PDF 45**

Graduated pricing has also been put in place for all usages. Users struggled at the beginning to stay in the boundaries of their budget but now that many habits and methods are in place to keep volumes under control, there is very little need to plan for additional cost and basic needs are fulfilled at a very low cost.

**G242:p0045:c003 · PDF 45**

Electricity Supply

**G242:p0045:c004 · PDF 45**

All enterprises have classified their digital usage. In the event of supply tensions on the electricity network, and upon request of the operators, organizations can reduce their electricity needs while maintaining vital activities. This has been also useful to mitigate the rise of electricity costs.

**G242:p0045:c005 · PDF 45**

Operating System Management

**G242:p0045:c006 · PDF 45**

They are now open source, very stable and maintained by large, motivated communities of developers for 10 years minimum. This stability was a high benefit for all the enterprises that can now dedicate their IT resources to improve their business-specific development and for security. It has also significantly decreased the need for cybersecurity as systems are now secured with mature technics and there is no more security breach coming from non-updated operating systems or new code.

**G242:p0045:c007 · PDF 45**

Software

**G242:p0045:c008 · PDF 45**

The mandatory maintenance period by editors has increased over time to now reach 20 years, which is aligned with the servers’ guarantee period. There has been a lot of difficult discussions with the industry software to reach this state, but it was a critical point to extend time usages of equipment. Moreover, when publishing a software, the delivery on an open-source platform is mandatory so it can be made public either at the end of the guarantee period or if the software company stops its activity.

**G242:p0045:c009 · PDF 45**

Data Management

**G242:p0045:c010 · PDF 45**

Any new structured or unstructured data is labeled with an owner, business glossary index, and an expiry date allowing automated deletion and stabilization of storage volumes. Some data is labeled for a final storage and is archived on low energy intensive backup solutions in an open standard format that secures future accessibility.

**G242:p0045:c011 · PDF 45**

Digital Services

**G242:p0045:c012 · PDF 45**

Digital Services design is now optimizing data storage, electricity consumption, network communication and processing, which allows a very high level of accessibility.

**G242:p0046:c000 · PDF 46**

Half of digital services are open source, and the other half is now served by operators that have a business model relying on the quality of service and not the volumes of data.

**G242:p0046:c001 · PDF 46**

They all embed some features that encourage minimal usage and raise alerts when data volume quotas and time usages are exceeded. They were designed to contribute to the end of digital addiction problems. This change was supported by marketing campaigns at the beginning but now that habits have been created, they are not needed anymore.

**G242:p0046:c002 · PDF 46**

Open Data

**G242:p0046:c003 · PDF 46**

Free Open Data available have been multiplied by 1000 and they are growing by 5% per year. Their management is performed by international and national government-funded agencies. This enables high levels of interoperability while reducing the storage needs of each organization.

**G242:p0046:c004 · PDF 46**

The Science-Based Targets (SBT) initiative [Science-Based Targets] has scaled and science-based environmental footprint and targets are now certified for most organizations by an independent international organization supported by United Nations, maintain a repository of these targets.

**G242:p0046:c005 · PDF 46**

Measure of ICT Environmental Impact

**G242:p0046:c006 · PDF 46**

The environmental footprint data (GHG emissions, electricity consumption, water consumption, impact on biomass and biodiversity) is now tracked with the same level of consideration as finance and is available for any product, service or even enterprise. Standards and free reference-shared data are used worldwide, and agencies controlling the data quality are in place in most countries.

**G242:p0046:c007 · PDF 46**

Thanks to this shift, ICT environmental impact is now compatible with carbon neutrality targets in most countries, while providing high quality digital services and supporting intensive regeneration of the biosphere worldwide.

**G242:p0046:c008 · PDF 46**

Innovation

**G242:p0046:c009 · PDF 46**

The Artificial Intelligence (AI) and blockchain hype have been stopped by the historic electricity crisis and voluntary usage restrictions. Innovation is now dedicated to bio-digital solutions with the lowest possible environmental impact and energy consumption. The DNA data storage capability, batteries based on biopolymers that can be then recycled and burned as biomass fuel the hope of young generations for a fully sustainable IT.

</details>

<a id="3.5.2"></a>
## 以“用途 + 基础设施”双视角驱动转型

原文：3.5.2. A Dual Use-Based and Infrastructure-Based Architecture Approach to Drive Transformation。PDF 物理页：[46, 47, 48, 49, 50, 51, 52]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

向可持续信息系统转变是一项重大转型，将影响架构工作的全部范围。在令人向往的愿景之后，本章提出一种利用现有企业架构能力的具体方法来促成这一转变。

**减少 ICT 环境足迹的架构杠杆**

可持续信息系统的目标架构必须一致地重塑业务流程及相关系统、应用与数据的使用方式，以及支撑这些组件的技术基础设施。

1. 企业 ICT 系统的碳足迹由技术基础设施的碳足迹构成：设备组件、服务器、存储单元、网络控制器、屏幕、固定与移动设备，以及运行它们的技术软件。

   它不仅限于运行期间的用电，还包括建造、组件安装、维护与报废的能耗。

   它不仅限于本地产生的排放；还包括因多个组件的制造与多台服务器的运行而在境外产生的排放。

   它不仅限于公司自有的组件，还包括代表公司运营的 ICT（例如云）以及功能性分包商（例如广告代理）的组件排放。

2. 技术基础设施存在的唯一目的是存储、传输和处理数据，或运行应用软件。

3. ICT 系统还由业务流程刻画，这些流程以口头、书面与数字形式受控地使用信息；应用软件使业务流程中的部分程序自动化。

因此，ICT 负责人可以通过在多个层面调整设计来组织碳足迹的减少：

- 告知各部门其所申请数字服务的环境足迹，并提供倾向于高社会价值服务的选择；
- 优化业务流程，并用应用软件优化自动化程序；
- 优化应用层及其调用技术层的方式；
- 优化技术层。

**结合自上而下与自下而上的方法以捕获数字可持续性需求**

可以同时结合以下两种方法来识别可持续性需求：

- **基于业务用途的方法**，贴近业务运营者，目标是减少已识别业务用途的足迹（例如发送邮件、共享文档，或某个业务应用的整体实际使用）。

  如 3.6.3 节所述，按业务功能/业务应用层面汇总的初始足迹度量，可以在各业务用途之间确定优先级。

- **基于基础设施的方法**，目标是减少被识别为消耗资源和数据的 ICT 系统（可能承载多种用途）的足迹。

  同样，对现有基础设施的初始足迹度量应有助于识别高排放系统。

这两种方法汇合起来，可识别出一套完整的减缓行动：

- 基于业务用途的方法必须交付一份可放弃或可优化的业务用途优先级清单。它需要：

  - 对如何实现进行战略性思考——这可能因业务而异；事实上，过去 40 年组织一直处于持续的自动化进程中，这是一次重大的逆转；
  - 高层管理者的授权，以便与业务单元共同处理这些议题；
  - 与业务单元（系统的最终用户）进行交流；
  - 按业务单元或业务应用提供初始指标，以瞄准环境影响最大的用途。

- 基于基础设施的方法必须交付一份应淘汰或优化的数字设备优先级清单。它需要：

  - 高层管理者的授权，以便与基础设施团队共同处理这些议题；
  - 支撑组织信息系统的基础设备清单；
  - 关于基础设施的初始指标（例如足迹最高或人均足迹最高者），以瞄准环境影响最大的系统。

在两种情况下，淘汰机会与优化机会都可能存在于现有基础设施、开发、软件架构、数据，以及系统业务用途的真实定义之中。在这两种方法中，唯一变化的参数是切入点：优化某个业务用途需要“向下”走到技术系统；优化某个技术系统则需要“向上”走到其业务用途（例如确保业务单元能够接受系统缩减或优化可能带来的影响）。

落实从业务用途或从基础设施识别出的淘汰与优化机会，是构建排放更低的信息系统的设计主干。

**修订非功能需求**

总体而言，交付这些减缓与减少机会，必须导致对系统及新 ICT 项目中的非功能需求进行修订，其主要趋势见图 3-6。

敢于对信息系统及其使用施加约束，要求降低可用性、存储容量与能源需求。而对系统在长寿性、可维护性、可拆解性、可维修性、可移植性、互操作性与稳健性方面的要求，总体上将会提高。

除了逐个用途、逐个系统的减缓选项之外，重要的是对系统设置约束，以推动其长期走上低碳轨迹。

**设置约束**

设置约束可能被感知为不愉快，因此必须与文化建设方法和有吸引力的愿景相结合。对架构师而言，这是推动组织走向可持续的强有力且必不可少的杠杆：

- 生态系统正朝着对资源、法规等日益增加的约束方向发展。为避免在毫无准备的情况下于错误时机被约束，最好提前设想一个处于选定约束之下的系统，并相应地调整组织、流程、用途与系统。
- 建立一套约束体系还可以通过显式限制用途、基础设施与数据（数量、位置）来限制系统的反弹效应。

为此，系统必须逐步受到约束。由架构设置的约束必须涵盖多种形式：

- **对用途的约束**。例如：限制某些特定用途的用量（每人每天在邮件中发送的附件量、每天消费的视频量等），限制某些用途的频率（每人每天打开的网页数），或完全限制某些用途（限制访问社交媒体、流媒体网站、移动网络、大容量存储能力等）。
- **对数据的约束**。例如：为公司设定最大数据量、为每个用户/每个应用设定最大数据量，限制数据物理位置的地理范围（优先把数据存储在尽可能靠近最终用户的地方，或电网中分配低碳电力的区域）。
- **对应用与开发的约束**。例如：限制/约束某些框架、某些库的使用；约束高碳足迹技术的使用。对开发与测试增加约束，质疑“DevOps”的必要性——即持续运行一条沉重的持续集成与持续交付/部署（CI/CD）流水线。
- **对基础设施/设备的约束**。例如：限制所采购设备的类型、服务器数量、虚拟机（VM）/容器数量、所用技术对象的最大数量（按规模区间），并限制某些基础设施的能耗。

ICT 架构师必须让所有利益相关者参与，正式确定并验证一条通往“处于选定约束之下的系统”的路径。这项工作当然比看上去更复杂。它不是要强加“不明智”的约束。要能够设计一个受适当约束的系统，需要先建立度量。这意味着在不同层面组合约束，同时通过文化与意识确保它们对组织的业务流程也是可接受、可承受的。

约束必须显式表达、说明理由，并在治理活动的框架内定期评审，其方式与架构原则相同。

**表 3-3：设置与治理约束**

| 约束 | 理由 | 使系统保持合规的复杂度 | 遵守约束的价值 | 约束能否自动化（自动阻断/告警消息等） | 合规控制方式（如何检查约束被遵守） |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 云端存储的数据量不得超过 x TO | … | … | … |  |  |
| 数据必须物理存放在使用国境内 |  |  |  |  |  |

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 结合从业务用途出发与从基础设施出发的架构分析。
  - 适用边界：两个方向必须汇合为同一业务需求和架构权衡，不产生互不一致的目标模型。
